{{{{{}}{
{}{}}{}{}{{}{}}}}}{}{{{{{}}{
{}{}}{}{}{{}{}}}}}{{}}{}{}{}{}{}{{{}{}}}{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{}{}{{{}{}}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.39998pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=2.33746pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{}{{}}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -23.98 -0.15 L 15.08 -0.8}{fill:none} \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\pgfinnerlinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=1.5375pt} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#FFFFFF} \lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -23.98 -0.15 L 15.08 -0.8}{fill:none} }\lxSVG@closescope {{}{{}}{}{}{{}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}}{{}{{}}{}{}{{}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@transformcm{0.99986}{-0.01656}{0.01656}{0.99986}{10.8955pt}{-0.57854pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform=matrix(0.99986 -0.01656 0.01656 0.99986 15.08 -0.8)} \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.39998pt} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-dasharray=none,stroke-dashoffset=0.0pt} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linecap=round} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin=round} \lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -1.8 3.55 C -0.92 1.68 1.42 0.07 2.76 0 C 1.42 -0.07 -0.92 -1.68 -1.8 -3.55}{fill:none} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@closescope }}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lxSVG@closescope \hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{}{}{{
{}{}}}{
{}{}}
{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-8.85004pt}{-5.72954pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform=matrix(1.0 0.0 0.0 1.0 -12.25 -7.93)} \pgfsys@hbox{58}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}

similar to that of a
2-coend

[
Lor21
]
.
At the unit
{\cal D}={\cal I}
, we of course have

\operatorname{ev}_{\cal I}=1_{\cal I}=\operatorname{cev}_{\cal I}

through the unitors
r_{\cal I},\ell_{\cal I}=1_{\cal I}
. A similar construction with the inverse antipode
\tilde{S}^{-1}
yields the left-folds

\overline{\operatorname{ev}}_{\cal D}:{\cal D}\boxtimes\,^{*}{\cal D}\rightarrow{\cal I},\qquad\overline{\operatorname{cev}}_{\cal D}:{\cal I}\rightarrow\,^{*}{\cal D}\boxtimes{\cal D}.

We shall without loss of essential generality focus on the right-duals in the following.

Remark 3.4
.

The reason we can just focus on the right-duals is the following. By the hypothesis that
\tilde{S}
is an equivalence and
\,{}^{*}{\cal D}^{*}\cong{\cal D}
, the right-folds admit invertible
\tilde{C}
-module natural transformations

\overline{\operatorname{ev}}_{{\cal D}^{*}}\cong\operatorname{ev}_{\cal D},\qquad\overline{\operatorname{cev}}_{{\cal D}^{*}}\cong\operatorname{cev}_{\cal D}.

This allows us to transport all arguments that we shall make for the right-dual/folds to the left-dual/folds, but this does
not
force the left- and right-duals to coincide. Another subtlety is that we should not treat this property as giving the left-duality datum
{\cal D}^{*}
given the right-duality datum of
{\cal D}
; this will be elaborated more in §
4.2
.

\Diamond

3.3.2
Folds on tensor products

Now consider the fold map
\operatorname{ev}_{{\cal D}\boxtimes{\cal D}^{\prime}}:({\cal D}\boxtimes{\cal D}^{\prime})^{*}\boxtimes({\cal D}\boxtimes{\cal D}^{\prime})\rightarrow{\cal I}
. We can also achieve a map of this form by using the property
({\cal D}\boxtimes{\cal D}^{\prime})^{*}={\cal D}^{\prime*}\boxtimes{\cal D}^{*}
. Indeed, the following series of 1-morphisms gives

\displaystyle({\cal D}^{\prime*}\boxtimes{\cal D}^{*})\boxtimes({\cal D}\boxtimes{\cal D}^{\prime})\xrightarrow{\alpha^{\tilde{C}}_{({\cal D}^{\prime*}\boxtimes{\cal D}^{*}){\cal D}{\cal D}^{\prime}}}{\cal D}^{\prime*}\boxtimes({\cal D}^{*}\boxtimes({\cal D}\boxtimes{\cal D}^{\prime}))

\displaystyle\qquad\xrightarrow{{\cal D}^{*}\boxtimes(\alpha^{\tilde{C}}_{{\cal D}^{\prime*}{\cal D}^{\prime}{\cal D}})^{-1}}{\cal D}^{\prime*}\boxtimes(({\cal D}^{*}\boxtimes{\cal D})\boxtimes{\cal D}^{\prime})\xrightarrow{{\cal D}^{\prime*}\boxtimes\operatorname{ev}_{{\cal D}}\boxtimes{\cal D}^{\prime}}{\cal D}^{\prime*}\boxtimes({\cal I}\boxtimes{\cal D}^{\prime})

\displaystyle\qquad\xrightarrow{{\cal D}^{\prime*}\boxtimes\ell_{{\cal D}^{\prime}}}{\cal D}^{\prime*}\boxtimes{\cal D}^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\operatorname{ev}_{{\cal D}^{\prime}}}{\cal I}.

By construction, this functor admits the same
\tilde{C}
-module structure as
\operatorname{ev}_{{\cal D}\boxtimes{\cal D}^{\prime}}
; similar computations hold for the other folds. The
fold condition
then states that these functors coincide on-the-nose. Suppressing the associators and the unitors, we thus get

\operatorname{ev}_{{\cal D}\boxtimes{\cal D}^{\prime}}=\operatorname{ev}_{{\cal D}^{\prime}}\circ({\cal D}^{\prime*}\boxtimes\operatorname{ev}_{{\cal D}}\boxtimes{\cal D}^{\prime}),\qquad\operatorname{cev}_{{\cal D}\boxtimes{\cal D}^{\prime}}=({\cal D}\boxtimes\operatorname{cev}_{{\cal D}^{\prime}}\boxtimes{\cal D}^{*})\circ\operatorname{cev}_{{\cal D}}.

(3.11)

We now prove a consistency formula for the mates
F^{*},\,^{*}F
of a functor
F:{\cal D}\rightarrow{\cal D}^{\prime}
.

Proposition 3.6
.

There are invertible
\tilde{C}
-module natural transformations that identify the left- and right-mates

\displaystyle F^{*}\cong\ell_{{\cal D}^{*}}\circ(\operatorname{ev}_{{\cal D}^{\prime}}\boxtimes{\cal D}^{*})\circ({\cal D}^{\prime*}\boxtimes F\boxtimes{\cal D}^{*})\circ({\cal D}^{\prime*}\boxtimes\operatorname{cev}_{{\cal D}})\circ r_{{\cal D}^{\prime*}}^{-1},

\,{}^{*}F\cong r_{\,{}^{*}{\cal D}}\circ(\,^{*}{\cal D}\boxtimes\overline{\operatorname{ev}}_{{\cal D}^{\prime}})\circ(\,^{*}{\cal D}\boxtimes F\boxtimes\,^{*}{\cal D}^{\prime})\circ(\overline{\operatorname{cev}}_{{\cal D}}\boxtimes\,^{*}{\cal D}^{\prime})\circ\ell_{\,{}^{*}{\cal D}^{\prime}}^{-1}

of a functor
F:{\cal D}\rightarrow{\cal D}^{\prime}
.

Proof.

We can use the above fold maps to construct a functor
F^{\prime*}:{\cal D}^{\prime*}\rightarrow{\cal D}^{*}
by

F^{\prime*}=\ell_{{\cal D}^{*}}\circ(\operatorname{ev}_{{\cal D}^{\prime}}\boxtimes{\cal D})\circ({\cal D}^{\prime*}\boxtimes F\boxtimes{\cal D}^{*})\circ({\cal D}^{\prime*}\boxtimes\operatorname{cev}_{{\cal D}})\circ r_{{\cal D}^{\prime*}}^{-1},

where we have used the invertible associator 2-morphism arising from the coassociativity of
\tilde{\Delta}_{1}
to disambiguiate the middle factor

\alpha_{{\cal D}^{\prime*}F{\cal D}^{*}}^{-1}:{\cal D}^{\prime*}\boxtimes(F\boxtimes{\cal D}^{*})\Rightarrow({\cal D}^{\prime*}\boxtimes F)\boxtimes{\cal D}^{*}.

By construction,
F^{*},F^{\prime*}
takes the same values on objects, hence it suffices to prove that they have the same
{\cal E}
-structure (
3.1
). Recall
F^{*}
inherits a
{\cal E}
-structure given by
\tilde{S}_{1}\gamma_{e}
. The computation using the antipode (
2.2
) and counit axioms lead to a series of natural isomorphisms

\displaystyle(-\times-\otimes 1)\circ(\tilde{S}_{1}\otimes

\displaystyle 1\otimes\tilde{S}_{1})\circ(\tilde{\Delta}_{1}\otimes 1)\circ\tilde{\Delta}_{1}

\displaystyle=(((-\times-)\circ(\tilde{S}_{1}\otimes 1)\circ\tilde{\Delta}_{1})\otimes 1)\circ((1\otimes\tilde{S}_{1})\circ\tilde{\Delta}_{1}

\displaystyle=(\tilde{\epsilon}_{1}\cdot 1\otimes 1)\circ((1\otimes\tilde{S}_{1})\circ\tilde{\Delta})

\displaystyle=\tilde{S}_{1}\circ(\tilde{\epsilon}_{1}\otimes 1)\circ\tilde{\Delta}=\tilde{S}_{1}\circ\operatorname{id},

which determines the same
{\cal E}
-structure as
F^{\prime*}
. This gives the
\tilde{C}
-module functor identification
F^{*}\cong F^{\prime*}
. The same argument with
\tilde{S}^{-1}
works for the left-mate.
∎

By computing the double dual-mate
F^{**}
in two different ways, we can deduce

\operatorname{ev}^{*}_{{\cal D}}\cong\operatorname{cev}_{{\cal D}^{*}},\qquad\operatorname{cev}^{*}_{\cal D}\cong\operatorname{ev}_{{\cal D}^{*}}.

(3.12)

Note this does
not
determine the object-level pre-duality datum, since
\operatorname{ev}_{F}
is only defined for endofunctors.

A pivotality condition.

Recall that we have the identification
\,{}^{*}{\cal D}^{*}\cong{\cal D}
, thus if we replace
{\cal D}
by its left-dual
{\cal D}^{*}
, then the above proposition allows us to write the
right
-mate of a functor
F:{\cal D}^{*}\rightarrow{\cal D}^{\prime*}
as

\,{}^{*}F\cong r_{{\cal D}}\circ({\cal D}\boxtimes\overline{\operatorname{ev}}_{{\cal D}^{\prime*}})\circ({\cal D}\boxtimes F\boxtimes\,^{*}{\cal D}^{\prime})\circ(\overline{\operatorname{cev}}_{{\cal D}^{*}}\boxtimes{\cal D}^{\prime})\circ\ell_{{\cal D}^{\prime}}^{-1}:{\cal D}^{\prime}\rightarrow{\cal D}.

If we further impose a condition in which the left-dual is involutive
({\cal D}^{*})^{*}\cong{\cal D}
such that the left- and right-folds coincide, then we acquire a pivotality condition
\,{}^{*}{\cal D}\cong{\cal D}^{*}
, and the above formula recovers the one given in pg. 49 of
[
DR18
]
(modulo the convention in which the duals are folded).

Of course, this condition in general does not hold unless
\tilde{S}^{2}\cong 1_{\tilde{C}}
is unipotent — namely
\tilde{C}
itself has a "copivotal" structure. This is why
\operatorname{2Rep}(\tilde{C};\tilde{R})
is not a bona fide pivotal 2-category in the sense of
[
DR18
]
— the failure is measured by the difference between the notions of left- and right-duality
{\cal D}^{*},\,^{*}{\cal D}
. We will analyze this issue in much greater detail in §
4.2
and §
6
.

Remark 3.5
.

Note
\tilde{S}=\tilde{S}
is indeed unipotent in the undeformed/classical case, as it is merely given by the (horizontal) inversion
\zeta\mapsto\zeta^{-1}
of 2-gauge parameters. Here, the 2-
R
-matrix
\tilde{R}=\operatorname{id}\otimes\operatorname{id}
is simply the unit, hence it would be possible for
\operatorname{2Rep}(\mathbb{U}_{q=0}\mathfrak{G};\operatorname{id}\otimes\operatorname{id})
to be pivotal. This situation is similar to many well-known examples of compact quantum groups
[
Maj11
,
Wor88
]
: the quantum deformation destroys the symmetry of their representation categories.

\Diamond

3.3.3
snakerators/cusps/cusps of the left dual

An immediate consequence of
Proposition
3.6
is the following. Setting
F=1_{\cal D}\in\operatorname{End}({\cal D})
, we obtain a formula for the left- and right-mates of the identity

\displaystyle 1_{\cal D}^{*}\cong\ell_{{\cal D}^{*}}\circ(\operatorname{ev}_{{\cal D}}\boxtimes{\cal D}^{*})\circ({\cal D}^{*}\boxtimes\operatorname{cev}_{{\cal D}})\circ r_{{\cal D}^{*}}^{-1},

\,{}^{*}1_{\cal D}\cong r_{\,{}^{*}{\cal D}}\circ(\,^{*}{\cal D}\boxtimes\overline{\operatorname{ev}}_{{\cal D}})\circ(\overline{\operatorname{cev}}_{{\cal D}}\boxtimes\,^{*}{\cal D})\circ\ell_{\,{}^{*}{\cal D}}^{-1}.

However, we know from (
3.10
) that these are in fact formulas for
1_{{\cal D}^{*}},1_{\,{}^{*}{\cal D}}
. The identification
{\cal D}\cong\,^{*}{\cal D}^{*}
and
Remark
3.4
then allow us to define the following invertible 2-morphisms (neglecting the invertible associators and unitors)

\varrho_{{\cal D}}:1_{{\cal D}^{*}}\Rightarrow(\operatorname{ev}_{{\cal D}}\boxtimes{\cal D}^{*})\circ({\cal D}^{*}\boxtimes\operatorname{cev}_{{\cal D}}),\qquad\varphi_{\cal D}:({\cal D}\boxtimes\operatorname{ev}_{{\cal D}})\circ({\operatorname{cev}}_{{\cal D}}\boxtimes{\cal D})\Rightarrow 1_{{\cal D}},

(3.13)

which we call the
snakerators/cusps
for the folds. It is then easy to see that

\varphi_{\cal I}=\operatorname{id}_{1_{\cal I}},\qquad\varrho_{{\cal I}}=\operatorname{id}_{1_{\cal I}}

are identity 2-morphisms.

Proposition 3.7
.

There are 2-morphisms such that

a_{\cal D}:\,^{*}\operatorname{ev}_{\cal D}\Rightarrow\overline{\operatorname{cev}}_{\cal D},\qquad b_{\cal D}:\overline{\operatorname{ev}}_{\cal D}\Rightarrow\,^{*}\operatorname{cev}_{\cal D}.

Proof.

First, from
Remark
3.4
and (
3.11
), we have

\operatorname{cev}_{\,{}^{*}{\cal D}\boxtimes{\cal D}}=(\,^{*}{\cal D}\boxtimes\operatorname{cev}_{\cal D}\boxtimes{\cal D})\circ\operatorname{cev}_{\,{}^{*}{\cal D}}\cong(\,^{*}{\cal D}\boxtimes\operatorname{cev}_{\cal D}\boxtimes{\cal D})\circ\overline{\operatorname{cev}}_{{\cal D}}.

Then from the fact that
\overline{\operatorname{ev}}_{{\cal I}}\cong 1_{\cal I}
through the invertible unitors,
Proposition
3.6
gives

\,{}^{*}\operatorname{ev}_{\cal D}

\displaystyle\cong(\,^{*}({\cal D}^{*}\boxtimes{\cal D})\boxtimes\operatorname{ev}_{\cal D}\boxtimes{\cal I})\circ(\overline{\operatorname{cev}}_{\,{}^{*}{\cal D}\boxtimes{\cal D}}\boxtimes{\cal I})

\displaystyle=\,^{*}{\cal D}\boxtimes\big(({\cal D}\boxtimes\operatorname{ev}_{\cal D})\circ(\operatorname{cev}_{\cal D}\boxtimes{\cal D})\big)\circ\overline{\operatorname{cev}}_{\cal D},

which by (
3.13
) admits a snakerator
\,{}^{*}{\cal D}\boxtimes\varphi_{\cal D}\circ\overline{\operatorname{cev}}_{\cal D}
into

\,{}^{*}{\cal D}\boxtimes 1_{\cal D}\circ\overline{\operatorname{cev}}_{\cal D}=\overline{\operatorname{cev}}_{\cal D},

as desired. A similar argument can be applied to
\,{}^{*}\operatorname{cev}_{\cal D}
by using the snakerator
\varrho_{\cal D}
.
∎

Now consider the tensor product
{\cal D}\boxtimes{\cal A}
and the snakerator
\varphi_{{\cal D}\boxtimes{\cal A}}
. The domain of this 2-morphism is a 1-morphism

{\cal D}\boxtimes{\cal A}\rightarrow({\cal D}\boxtimes{\cal A})\boxtimes({\cal D}\boxtimes{\cal A})^{*}\boxtimes({\cal D}\boxtimes{\cal A})\rightarrow{\cal D}\boxtimes{\cal A},

in which the object in the centre is
({\cal D}\boxtimes{\cal A})\boxtimes({\cal A}^{*}\boxtimes{\cal D}^{*})\boxtimes({\cal D}\boxtimes{\cal A})
. By naturality, this object fits into a diagram of the form (here we have neglected the associators and the symbol
\boxtimes
to save space)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\cal D}{\cal A}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\cal D}{\cal D}^{*}{\cal D}{\cal A}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\cal D}{\cal A}{\cal A}^{*}{\cal D}^{*}{\cal D}{\cal A}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\cal D}{\cal A}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\cal D}{\cal A}{\cal A}^{*}{\cal A}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\cal D}{\cal A}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{cev}}_{{\cal D}}{\cal D}{\cal A}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1_{{\cal D}{\cal A}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\cal D}{\operatorname{cev}}_{{\cal A}}{\cal D}^{*}{\cal D}{\cal A}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\cal D}{\operatorname{ev}}_{{\cal D}}{\cal A}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\cal D}{\cal A}{\cal A}^{*}{\operatorname{ev}}_{{\cal D}}{\cal A}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\cal D}{\operatorname{cev}}_{{\cal A}}{\cal A}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1_{{\cal D}{\cal A}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\cal D}{\cal A}{\operatorname{ev}}_{{\cal A}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{{\cal D}^{*}}{\cal A}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\cal D}\varphi_{{\cal A}^{*}}}

in which the 2-cell in the middle is filled by the interchanger
{\cal D}\upsilon_{{\operatorname{cev}}_{{\cal A}},{\operatorname{ev}}_{{\cal D}}}{\cal A}
. In other words, we have the following formula

\varphi_{{\cal D}{\cal A}}=(\varphi_{\cal D}{\cal A}\circ{\cal D}\varphi_{\cal A})\bullet({\operatorname{ev}}_{{\cal D}}{\cal D}{\cal A}\circ{\cal D}\upsilon_{{\operatorname{cev}}_{{\cal A}},{\operatorname{ev}}_{{\cal D}}}{\cal A}\circ{\cal D}{\cal A}{\operatorname{cev}}_{{\cal A}}).

Similar arguments lead to the formula

\varrho_{{\cal D}{\cal A}}=({\cal A}^{*}{\cal D}^{*}{\operatorname{ev}}_{{\cal D}}\circ{\cal A}^{*}\upsilon_{{\operatorname{ev}}_{{\cal D}},{\operatorname{cev}}_{{\cal A}}}{\cal D}^{*}\circ{\operatorname{cev}}_{{\cal A}}{\cal A}^{*}{\cal D}^{*})\bullet({\cal A}^{*}\varrho_{\cal D}\circ\varrho_{\cal A}{\cal D}^{*}).

3.3.4
The swallowtail 2-morphisms

Consider the identity natural transformation on the fold
\operatorname{id}_{\operatorname{ev}_{\cal D}}:\operatorname{ev}_{\cal D}\Rightarrow\operatorname{ev}_{\cal D}
, whose legs are
{\cal D}^{*}{\cal D}\rightarrow{\cal I}
. Notice that there are two ways in which to write the identity functor
1_{{\cal D}\boxtimes{\cal D}^{*}}=1_{\cal D}\boxtimes{\cal D}^{*}={\cal D}\boxtimes 1_{{\cal D}^{*}}
. From (
3.13
), the former has a cusp
{\cal D}^{*}\varphi_{\cal D}
into it and the latter has a cusp
\varrho_{\cal D}{\cal D}
out of it. The consistency of these two expressions for the cusps are mediated by the interchanger, as can be seen in the following diagram

Similarly, the identity natural transform
\operatorname{id}_{\operatorname{cev}_{\cal D}}:\operatorname{cev}_{\cal D}\Rightarrow\operatorname{cev}_{\cal D}
has each of it legs given by
{\cal I}\rightarrow{\cal D}{\cal D}^{*}
. The identity functor
1_{{\cal D}{\cal D}^{*}}={\cal D}1_{{\cal D}^{*}}=1_{{\cal D}}{\cal D}^{*}
leads to the following

\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}.

The commutativity of these diagrams of 2-morphisms, namely the equations

\mathcal{S}_{\cal D}=\operatorname{id}_{1_{{\cal D}\boxtimes{\cal D}^{*}}},\qquad\mathcal{S}^{\prime}_{\cal D}=\operatorname{id}_{1_{{\cal D}^{*}\boxtimes{\cal D}}},

are known as the
swallowtail equations
; see C1, Definition 2.2.4 in
[
DR18
]
, as well as fig. 19 (c,d) in
[
BMS24
]
. However, we shall impose a more general condition involving a larger diagram of 2-morphisms, which "pastes" these swallowtail diagrams together using the braiding structure
c
. This condition is explained in §
5
.

So far, the above sections dealt with structures that have been known for some time. They were studied mainly in the context of
spherical
2-categories
[
Mac00
,
DR18
,
JR23
]
, but the fact that the left- and right-duals do not coincide means that
\operatorname{2Rep}(\tilde{C};\tilde{R})
is not pivotal, and hence cannot be spherical. In the following, we will work to analyze its structures more thoroughly.

4
Rigid dagger tensor 2-category
\operatorname{2Rep}(\tilde{C};\tilde{R})

Let us now return to
\operatorname{2Rep}(\tilde{C};\tilde{R})
and investigate the interplay between its duality and adjunction. Recall in §
3.2
that we have introduced a notion of
planar-unitarity
. The natural condition to impose is then to ask for all of the structural functors, eg. the associators
\alpha
, unitors
r_{\cal D},\ell_{\cal D}
, and particularly the folds
\operatorname{ev}_{\cal D},\operatorname{cev}_{\cal D}
, to be planar-unitary.

Following the definition of a
rigid
dagger tensor category in
[
JP17
]
, we propose the following definition.

Definition 4.1
.

A
rigid dagger tensor 2-category
is a tensor 2-category with adjoints equipped with (natural) left-duals
{\cal D}^{*}
for each object
{\cal D}
such that

1.

there exists a "left-dual"
\,{}^{*}{\cal D}
such that it is the pre-dual of the right-dual,
\,{}^{*}{\cal D}^{*}\cong{\cal D}
, and

2.

all folds
\operatorname{ev}_{\cal D},\operatorname{cev}_{{\cal D}}
and snakerators/cusps
\varphi_{\cal D},\varrho_{\cal D}
are planar-unitary.

It is sensible to impose these conditions on
\operatorname{2Rep}(\tilde{C};\tilde{R})
, as its duals and adjunctions — which we recall are defined by the antipode and the dagger structure
\tilde{S},\tilde{S}_{v}=-^{\dagger}
— are compatible.

\tilde{S}^{\text{op}}\circ\tilde{S}_{v}=\tilde{S}_{v}^{\text{m-op,c-op}}\circ\tilde{S}.

The goal in this section is to exhibit the coherence relations which make this definition more transparent.

Remark 4.1
.

Note that in
[
DM82
]
, the definition of a "rigid tensor category" has reflexitivity built in: there are isomorphisms
D\cong(D^{*})^{*}
trivializing the double dual of every object
D
. We do not a priori assume this property for
\operatorname{2Rep}(\tilde{C};\tilde{R})
, but we shall see in §
6.1.3
that those objects which
are
reflexive have particular geometric properties.

\Diamond

4.1
Compatibility between the folds

We begin by noting that duality and adjunctions
strongly
commute on endofunctors
F:{\cal D}\rightarrow{\cal D}
,

(F^{\dagger})^{*}=(F^{*})^{\dagger},\qquad\,^{*}(F^{\dagger})=(\,^{*}F)^{\dagger},

(4.1)

which allows the following triangles to commute,

\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}

Since the bottom square commutes on the nose,
\operatorname{ev}_{1_{{\cal D}^{*}\boxtimes{\cal D}}},\operatorname{cev}_{1_{{\cal D}^{*}\boxtimes{\cal D}}}
are trivial whence we obtain the following compatibility between the left-folds and the adjunction-folds

\displaystyle\operatorname{ev}_{F^{\dagger}}\circ\operatorname{ev}_{F}=e_{F^{*}}\boxtimes e_{F},\qquad\operatorname{cev}_{F^{\dagger}}\circ\operatorname{cev}_{F}=\iota_{F}\boxtimes\iota_{F^{*}},

\displaystyle\operatorname{ev}_{F}\circ\operatorname{ev}_{F^{\dagger}}=(\iota_{F^{*}}\boxtimes\iota_{F})^{-1},\qquad\operatorname{cev}_{F}\circ\operatorname{cev}_{F^{\dagger}}=(e_{F}\boxtimes e_{F^{*}})^{-1},

(4.2)

where the second row of conditions can be deduced from swapping the order of the composition of
F,F^{\dagger}
in the central triangles above.

4.2
Defect decorations on closed surfaces

Consider the adjunction-folds on the left-fold maps,

\displaystyle e_{\operatorname{ev}_{\cal D}}:\operatorname{ev}_{\cal D}^{\dagger}\circ\operatorname{ev}_{\cal D}\Rightarrow 1_{{\cal D}^{*}\boxtimes{\cal D}},\qquad\iota_{\operatorname{ev}_{\cal D}}:1_{{\cal I}}\Rightarrow\operatorname{ev}_{\cal D}\circ\operatorname{ev}_{\cal D}^{\dagger},

\displaystyle e_{\operatorname{cev}_{\cal D}}:\operatorname{cev}_{\cal D}^{\dagger}\circ\operatorname{cev}_{\cal D}\Rightarrow 1_{{\cal I}},\qquad\iota_{\operatorname{cev}_{\cal D}}:1_{{\cal D}\boxtimes{\cal D}^{*}}\Rightarrow\operatorname{cev}_{\cal D}\circ\operatorname{cev}_{\cal D}^{\dagger}.

These were called respectively the "crotch", "birth-of-a-circle", "saddle", and "death-of-a-circle" 2-morphisms in
[
DR18
]
(see figs. 27 & 28 in
[
BL03
]
, and also fig. 19 (a,b) in
[
BMS24
]
); collectively, we shall refer to them as the
fold-on-folds
2-morphisms. These can be used to construct surface defects decorated with 2-morphisms in
\operatorname{2Rep}(\tilde{C};\tilde{R})
.

By naturality and planar-unitarity, these 2-morphisms admit an action by
\tilde{C}
-module endofunctors
F\in\operatorname{End}({\cal D})
via

\displaystyle e_{\operatorname{ev}_{\cal D}}\mapsto e_{\operatorname{ev}_{\cal D}}\bullet(\operatorname{ev}_{F}^{\dagger}\circ\operatorname{ev}_{F}),\qquad\iota_{\operatorname{ev}_{\cal D}}\mapsto(\operatorname{ev}_{F}\circ\operatorname{ev}_{F}^{\dagger})\bullet\iota_{\operatorname{ev}_{\cal D}},

\displaystyle e_{\operatorname{cev}_{\cal D}}\mapsto e_{\operatorname{cev}_{\cal D}}\bullet(\operatorname{cev}_{F}\circ\operatorname{cev}_{F}^{\dagger}),\qquad\iota_{\operatorname{cev}_{\cal D}}\mapsto(\operatorname{cev}_{F}^{\dagger}\circ\operatorname{cev}_{F})\bullet\iota_{\operatorname{cev}_{\cal D}}.

(4.3)

It is important to note here that since our left- and right-duals do not coincide,
\operatorname{cev}_{{\cal D}^{*}}:{\cal I}\rightarrow{\cal D}^{*}\boxtimes({\cal D}^{*})^{*}
is not parallel with
\operatorname{ev}_{\cal D}^{\dagger}
. We will see how this can be addressed through the ribbon balancings in §
6.1
.

Remark 4.2
.

Suppose for the moment that the left- and right-duals coincide, so that the duality is involutive. The fact that
\operatorname{cev}_{{\cal D}^{*}}:{\cal I}\rightarrow{\cal D}^{*}\boxtimes({\cal D}^{*})^{*}\cong{\cal D}^{*}\boxtimes{\cal D}
now is parallel with
\operatorname{ev}_{\cal D}^{\dagger}
allows us to impose (C5, C6 Definition 2.2.4,
[
DR18
]
)

\operatorname{ev}_{{\cal D}^{*}}=\operatorname{cev}_{\cal D}^{\dagger},\qquad\varphi_{{\cal D}^{*}}=\varrho_{\cal D}^{\dagger},

(4.4)

which states that adjunction intertwines the duality folds. Under these conditions, one can form the 2-morphism

\mathfrak{Dim}({\cal D})=e_{\operatorname{cev}_{{\cal D}^{*}}}\bullet\iota_{\operatorname{ev}_{{\cal D}}}:1_{\cal I}\Rightarrow 1_{\cal I},

defining the 2-categorical dimension of
{\cal D}
(see Definition 2.3.8 of
[
DR18
]
) assigned to a
{\cal D}
-decorated closed 2-sphere. However, this 2-morphism
\mathfrak{Dim}({\cal D})
admits an action by
\operatorname{End}({\cal D})
according to (
4.3
), hence we see that the value of this 2-sphere is in general
not
invariant under taking equivalence classes of
{\cal D}
.

\Diamond

Pairing conventions.

We pause here to give a brief comment regarding the pairing conventions. In the case of the ordinary rigid (pivotal) tensor 1-category
C
(cf.
[
Eti+16
,
Bar16
]
), the folds define the duality datum
(c^{\dagger},e_{c},\iota_{c})
of an object
c\in C
. By pivotality, the pre-duality datum for
c^{\dagger}
can be uniquely determined by fixing the value of the quantum dimension,

\operatorname{dim}(c)^{2}=(e_{c}\circ\iota_{c^{\dagger}})\otimes(\iota_{c}\circ e_{c^{\dagger}}),

such that
(c^{\dagger},e_{c^{\dagger}}=\iota_{c}^{\dagger},\iota_{c^{\dagger}}=e_{c}^{\dagger})
. This is the planar-pivotal pairing convention we have used for the hom-categories of
\operatorname{2Rep}(\tilde{C};\tilde{R})
in §
3.2.2
. One of course wishes to adopt an analogous pairing convention for the object-level duality. However, as we have noted above, the 2-categorical dimension
\mathfrak{Dim}({\cal D})
cannot be computed naturally without the pivotality condition (
4.4
), and even if we do have pivotality, the issues mentioned in
Remark
4.2
makes it not clear if the pre-duality datum for
{\cal D}^{*}
enforced by (
4.4
) is unique. The most we have access to are the "fold-on-fold 2-morphisms" defined above in §
4.2
; we will use them in §
6.2
.

5
Braiding and rigidity

We now include the braiding into our analysis in this section, which shall lead to a natural notion of a rigid braided 2-category. Moreover, we shall define the ribbon twist from the braiding and relate this construction to the over-/under-twists defined in
[
DR18
]
. This will then guide us to propose a notion of a
ribbon tensor 2-category
, which are known to play major roles in both the physics of 4d TQFTs and the mathematics of 2-tangles
[
BL03
]
.

5.1
Braiding and adjunctions; the second Reidemeister move

Recall that adjunctions on
\operatorname{2Rep}(\tilde{C};\tilde{R})
is induced by an involution
\tilde{C}\rightarrow\tilde{C}^{\text{op}}
on
\tilde{C}
. This involution is contravariant, and hence only effects the cocomposition in
\tilde{C}
. Denoting the image of
\tilde{C}
under this involution by
\overline{C}=(\tilde{C})^{\text{op}}
, it is clear that
\overline{C}
is equipped with a cobraiding given by

\overline{\tilde{R}}\cong-^{\dagger}\circ\tilde{R}\circ-^{\dagger}.

We shall write
\overline{R}
also for the associated 2-
R
-matrix induced by the cobraiding via
Remark
2.3
.

We are now in the position to investigate the planar-unitarity of the braiding. Let
{\cal D},{\cal A}\in\operatorname{2Rep}(\tilde{C};\tilde{R})
. We define the adjoint of the braiding functor
c_{{\cal D},{\cal A}}^{\dagger}:{\cal A}\boxtimes{\cal D}\Rightarrow{\cal D}\boxtimes{\cal A}
to be the action by
\overline{R}
,

\displaystyle c_{{\cal D},{\cal A}}^{\dagger}=\text{flip}\circ(\rho_{\cal D}\otimes\rho_{\cal A})(\overline{R}),

\displaystyle c_{F,{\cal A}}^{\dagger}=\text{flip}\circ(\rho_{\cal D}\otimes\rho_{\cal A})(\overline{R}_{1}^{l}),\qquad c_{{\cal D},F^{\prime}}^{\dagger}=\text{flip}\circ(\rho_{\cal D}\otimes\rho_{\cal A})(\overline{R}_{1}^{r}),

where
F:{\cal D}\rightarrow{\cal D}^{\prime},F^{\prime}:{\cal A}\rightarrow{\cal A}^{\prime}
.

Now recall that
\tilde{S}_{v}=-^{\dagger}
implements an orientation reversal. This geometric understanding implies the existence of invertible 2-morphisms

e_{c_{{\cal D},{\cal A}}}:c_{{\cal D},{\cal A}}^{\dagger}\circ c_{{\cal D},{\cal A}}\Rightarrow 1_{{\cal D}\boxtimes{\cal A}},\qquad\iota_{c_{{\cal D},{\cal A}}}:1_{{\cal A}\boxtimes{\cal D}}\Rightarrow c_{{\cal D},{\cal A}}\circ c_{{\cal D},{\cal A}}^{\dagger}

(5.1)

for each
{\cal D},{\cal A}\in\operatorname{2Rep}(\tilde{C};\tilde{R})
. The structure of planar-unitarity for the braiding functos then identify (
5.1
) as the adjunction-folds on the braiding, which witness the
second Reidemeister move
(see fig. 26 in
[
BL03
]
, and fig. 55 (b) in
[
BMS24
]
). By the fact that the adjunction is involutive
(F^{\dagger})^{\dagger}\cong F
on
\tilde{C}
-module functors
F
, the (left-)adjunction-mates of these folds read

e_{c_{{\cal D},{\cal A}}}^{\dagger}:1_{{\cal D}\boxtimes{\cal A}}\Rightarrow c_{{\cal D},{\cal A}}^{\dagger}\circ c_{{\cal D},{\cal A}},\qquad\iota_{c_{{\cal D},{\cal A}}}^{\dagger}:1_{{\cal A}\boxtimes{\cal D}}\Rightarrow c_{{\cal D},{\cal A}}\circ c_{{\cal D},{\cal A}}^{\dagger}.

The definition of planar-unitarity then implies the unitarity of these adjunction-fold 2-morphisms. Similarly, the hexagonators
\Omega,\Omega^{\dagger}
are also unitary.

Remark 5.1
.

Note crucially that, despite the functors
c_{{\cal D},{\cal A}}^{\dagger},c_{{\cal A},{\cal D}}
are parallel, they are
not
2-isomorphic; we in general cannot even find a 2-morphism between them. However, if the 2-
R
-matrix
\tilde{R}
on
\tilde{C}
is
Hermitian
— that is, if it satisfies
\overline{R}=\tilde{R}^{T}
where
\tilde{R}^{t}=\sigma\tilde{R}
denotes the transposed 2-
R
-matrix — then we can indeed find a(n invertible)
\tilde{C}
-module natural transformation
c_{{\cal A},{\cal D}}\Rightarrow c_{{\cal D},{\cal A}}^{\dagger}
. Composing this with the fold
e_{c_{{\cal D},{\cal A}}}
then gives a 2-morphism which trivializes the
full braiding

C_{{\cal D},{\cal A}}=c_{{\cal A},{\cal D}}\circ c_{{\cal D},{\cal A}}\Rightarrow c_{{\cal D},{\cal A}}^{\dagger}\circ c_{{\cal D},{\cal A}}\Rightarrow 1_{{\cal D}\boxtimes{\cal A}}.

This makes
\operatorname{2Rep}(\tilde{C};\tilde{R})
sylleptic! Indeed, the syllepsis coherences follow directly from underlyinh
\tilde{C}
-module structures. In other words, a Hermitian 2-
R
-matrix gives rise to a sylleptic representation 2-category.

\Diamond

5.1.1
Braiding on the adjoints

Of course, for each
\tilde{C}
-module functor
F:{\cal D}\rightarrow{\cal D}^{\prime}
and
F^{\prime}:{\cal A}\rightarrow{\cal A}^{\prime}
we have, by naturalty, the following conditions

e_{c_{{\cal D}^{\prime},{\cal A}}}=e_{c_{{\cal D},{\cal A}}}\bullet(c_{F,{\cal A}}^{\dagger}\circ c_{F,{\cal A}}),\qquad\iota_{c_{{\cal D},{\cal A}^{\prime}}}=(c_{{\cal D},F^{\prime}}\circ c^{\dagger}_{{\cal D},F^{\prime}})\bullet\iota_{c_{{\cal D},{\cal A}}}.

However, together with the folds
e_{F}\boxtimes{\cal A}:F^{\dagger}\boxtimes{\cal A}\circ F\boxtimes{\cal A}\rightarrow 1_{{\cal D}\boxtimes{\cal A}}
, we can form the following diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\cal D}\boxtimes{\cal A}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\cal A}\boxtimes{\cal D}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\cal D}\boxtimes{\cal A}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\cal D}^{\prime}\boxtimes{\cal A}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\cal A}\boxtimes{\cal D}^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\cal D}^{\prime}\boxtimes{\cal A}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\cal D}\boxtimes{\cal A}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\cal A}\boxtimes{\cal D}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\cal D}\boxtimes{\cal A}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1_{{\cal D}\boxtimes{\cal A}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1_{{\cal D}\boxtimes{\cal A}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1_{{\cal D}\boxtimes{\cal A}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c_{F,{\cal A}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c_{F,{\cal A}}^{\dagger}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c_{F^{\dagger},{\cal A}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1_{{\cal D}\boxtimes{\cal A}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c_{F^{\dagger},{\cal A}}^{\dagger}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces e_{c_{{\cal D},{\cal A}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces e_{F}\boxtimes{\cal A}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces e_{F}\boxtimes{\cal A}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces e_{c_{{\cal D},{\cal A}}}}

which expresses the adjoints of the mixed braiding 2-morphisms
c_{F,{\cal A}}
; similarly for
c_{{\cal D},F^{\prime}}
.

To conclude that the 2-morphism
c_{F,{\cal A}}
is unitary, we must now exhibit a coherence relation between its two adjoints. This is done by super-imposing the top-right square in the above diagram by an orientation reversal of the bottom-left square, whence we achieve of the following commutative diagram of 2-morphisms

This can be written concisely using (
3.9
) as

e_{F\boxtimes{\cal A}\circ c_{{\cal D}\boxtimes{\cal A}}^{\dagger}}\bullet c_{F,{\cal A}}^{\dagger}\bullet\iota_{{\cal A}\boxtimes F^{\dagger}\circ c_{{\cal D}^{\prime}\boxtimes{\cal A}}}=\iota_{{\cal A}\boxtimes F^{\dagger}\circ c_{{\cal D}^{\prime}\boxtimes{\cal A}}}^{\dagger}\bullet c_{F^{\dagger},{\cal A}}\bullet e_{F\boxtimes{\cal A}\circ c_{{\cal D}\boxtimes{\cal A}}^{\dagger}}^{\dagger}.

(5.2)

From Definition 12 of
[
BL03
]
, we thus have the following.

Theorem 5.1
.

The rigid dagger tensor 2-category
\operatorname{2Rep}(\tilde{C};\tilde{R})
, equipped with planar-unitary braiding
c,
is a "braided monoidal 2-category with duals".

Note crucially that the notion of "duals" in this theorem refers to the adjunction
-^{\dagger}
, not actual object-level duality. This is because of the way the word "dual" is used in
[
BL03
]
is different from how we are using it here.

It is thus not possible to leverage the result in
[
Bae96a
]
and use adjunctions to construct a ribbon balancing/twist; we must use the object-level duality for this. This will be the subject of §
6.1
.

5.1.2
Adjunctions and higher-dagger structures

We pause here to elaborate more on the above comment. The object-level duality described in
[
BL03
]
— specifically for the "2-category of 2-tangles" — can in a sense be understood as a conflation of both the words "duals" and "adjoints" used in this paper and
[
DR18
]
: the functor adjoints coincide with their duality-mates. This can be attributed to the interpretation in
[
BL03
]
that the "generator of the 2-tangles" exist in the
unframed
universe. We now know how to refine this notion; indeed, it was conjectured in
[
Fer+24
]
(Conjecture 5.3) that the dagger-autoequivalences
\operatorname{Aut}^{\dagger}(\mathsf{AdjCat}_{(\infty,n)})
of
(\infty,n)
-categories with all adjoints is equivalent to the piecewise linear group
PL(n)
, away from
n=4
. The group
PL(n)
certainly does not just consist of one single duality
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
, unless
n=1
. This issue matters, as it underpins the cobordism hypothesis and the validity of graphical calculus.

The attentive reader may notice that the double delooping
B^{2}\operatorname{2Rep}(\tilde{C};\tilde{R})
has precisely
n=4
, for which the statement
PL(4)\simeq\operatorname{Aut}_{\text{sym}\otimes}(\mathsf{Bord}_{f}^{\text{fr}})
is equivalent to the open 4d PL Schoenflies conjecture
[
Lur08
]
. It thus seems that we must solve this open problem in geometric topology before being able to do graphical calculus with
\operatorname{2Rep}(\tilde{C};\tilde{R})
. However, this 2-category only has two adjoints, similar to the
\mathsf{Gray}
-categories with duals
[
BMS24
]
and the defect tricategories
[
CMS20
]
studied recently. As such, doing graphical calculus with
\operatorname{2Rep}(\tilde{C};\tilde{R})
may not be as sophisticated as the PL Schoenflies conjecture.

5.2
Braiding and duality

We begin our analysis here in a slightly different way as in the previous section. For each
{\cal D},{\cal A}\in\operatorname{2Rep}(\tilde{C};\tilde{R})
, the condition (
2.4
) can be seen to imply the existence of
\tilde{C}
-module 2-morphisms

({\cal D}^{*}\boxtimes c_{{\cal A},{\cal D}})\circ(c_{{\cal A},{\cal D}^{*}}\boxtimes{\cal D})\Rightarrow 1_{\cal A},\qquad 1_{\cal A}\Rightarrow({\cal D}\boxtimes c_{{\cal A},{\cal D}^{*}})\circ(c_{{\cal A},{\cal D}}\boxtimes{\cal D}^{*})

trivializing the subsequent braiding of
{\cal A}
against
{\cal D}
with its dual. These can be seen to arise from the following diagram,

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{({\cal A}{\cal D}^{*}){\cal D}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{({\cal D}^{*}{\cal A}){\cal D}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\cal D}^{*}({\cal A}{\cal D})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\cal D}^{*}({\cal D}{\cal A})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\cal A}({\cal D}^{*}{\cal D})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{({\cal D}^{*}{\cal D}){\cal A}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\cal A}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\cal A}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\cal A}({\cal D}{\cal D}^{*})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{({\cal D}{\cal D}^{*}){\cal A}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{({\cal A}{\cal D}){\cal D}^{*}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{({\cal D}{\cal A}){\cal D}^{*}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\cal D}({\cal A}{\cal D}^{*})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\cal D}({\cal D}^{*}{\cal A})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c_{{\cal A},{\cal D}^{*}}{\cal D}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega_{{\cal A}|{\cal D}^{*}{\cal D}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\cal D}^{*}c_{{\cal A},{\cal D}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c_{{\cal A},{\cal D}^{*}\boxtimes{\cal D}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1_{\cal A}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c_{{\cal A},{\cal D}\boxtimes{\cal D}^{*}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega_{{\cal A}|{\cal D}{\cal D}^{*}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c_{{\cal A}\boxtimes{\cal D}}{\cal D}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\cal D}c_{{\cal A},{\cal D}^{*}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c_{{\cal A},\operatorname{ev}_{\cal D}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c_{{\cal A},\operatorname{cev}_{\cal D}}}

in which these 2-morphisms arise from composing the unitary hexagonators
\Omega
with the 2-morphism

c_{\operatorname{ev}_{\cal D},{\cal A}}:c_{{\cal D}^{*}\boxtimes{\cal D},{\cal A}}\Rightarrow 1_{\cal A},\qquad c_{\operatorname{cev}_{\cal D},{\cal A}}:1_{\cal A}\rightarrow c_{{\cal D}\boxtimes{\cal D}^{*},{\cal A}}.

Together with the braid-exchange 2-morphism
c_{{\cal A},c_{{\cal D}^{*},{\cal D}}}
, this diagram can be seen as a trivialization of the 2-morphism
\Omega_{c_{{\cal A},{\cal D}^{*}}|{\cal D}}
for each
{\cal A},{\cal D}
. In other words, the presence of rigid duals in a braided monoidal 2-category allows us to trivialize the unitary hexagonators
\Omega_{-|--}
, as well as the 2-morphisms
\Omega_{c_{-,-}|-}
, whenever any two of the arguments in them are mutually dual.

We shall demonstrate in §
5.2.2
the compatibility of the braiding against both the rigid duality and the involutive
\dagger
-adjunctions, and the square in the centre of the above diagram will play an important role. To set up this exposition, we will first describe an important class of braiding structures.

5.2.1
Braiding structures on duals

For each
{\cal D}\in\operatorname{2Rep}(\tilde{C};\tilde{R})
and endofunctor
F:{\cal D}\rightarrow{\cal D}
, consider the braiding map
c_{{\cal D},{\cal D}^{*}}:{\cal D}\boxtimes{\cal D}^{*}\rightarrow{\cal D}^{*}\boxtimes{\cal D}
, given by the structures

\displaystyle c_{{\cal D},{\cal D}^{*}}=\text{flip}\circ(\rho\boxtimes\rho^{*})(\tilde{R}_{0})=\text{flip}\circ(\rho\boxtimes\rho)((1\otimes\tilde{S}_{0})\tilde{R}_{0}),

\displaystyle c_{F,{\cal D}^{*}}=\text{flip}\circ(\rho\boxtimes\rho)((1\otimes\tilde{S}_{1})\tilde{R}_{1}^{l}),\qquad c_{{\cal D},F^{*}}=\text{flip}\circ(\rho\boxtimes\rho)((1\otimes\tilde{S}_{0})\tilde{R}_{1}^{r}).

Through the module associator
(\rho(-)\otimes\rho(-))(-)\Rightarrow\rho(-\cdot-)(-)
, these braiding maps are determined by the following quantities

\nu=(-\cdot-)(\tilde{S}\otimes 1)\tilde{R}^{T}\equiv\nu^{l}\oplus\nu^{r},

in
\tilde{C}
, where the horizontal transpose 2-
R
-matrix
\tilde{R}^{T}
is intertwined by the flip map,

((\rho_{2}\otimes\rho_{1})\tilde{R}^{T}_{\mathrm{h}})\circ\text{flip}=\text{flip}\circ((\rho_{1}\otimes\rho_{2})\tilde{R}).

The naturality of the cobraiding transformation
\tilde{R}
implies the following
nudging equations

\nu^{l}\cdot\nu^{r}=\nu^{r}\cdot\nu^{l}.

(5.3)

Notice in the setting of weak 2-gauge theory (see
Remark
3.2
), the associator
\tau
representing the Postnikov class of
\mathbb{G}
will contribute to (
5.3
). Similar constructions can be made for the braiding
c_{{\cal D}^{*},{\cal D}}:{\cal D}^{*}\boxtimes{\cal D}\rightarrow{\cal D}\boxtimes{\cal D}^{*}
with the dual on the other side, which are associated to the following elements

\mu=(-\cdot-)(1\otimes\tilde{S})\tilde{R}^{T}

satisfying its own nudging equations.

Remark 5.2
.

This Hopf category object
\nu=(-\cdot-)(\tilde{S}\otimes 1)\tilde{R}^{T}
is a categorification of a key piece of structural data for Hopf algebras. It is known
[
Rad92
]
that for finite-dimensional quasitriangular Hopf algebras
H
, the analogous Hopf algebra element
\nu=m(S\otimes 1)R^{T}\in H
is invertible and represents the antipode-squared as an inner automorphism,

S^{2}(a)=\nu a\nu^{-1},\qquad\forall\penalty\ a\in H.

As such, the centrality
\nu\in Z(H)
implies that
S^{2}=\operatorname{id}
. This element also plays a central role in the proof of the Radford
S^{4}
-formula
[
Rad76
,
DDW06
]
. It would therefore be reasonable to posit that, if the Hopf category object
\nu
lift to the Drinfel’d centre
Z_{1}(\tilde{C})
,
8
8

8

Note
\tilde{C}
is only just cobraided, hence it makes sense to talk about its Drinfel’d centre.
then
\tilde{C}
is a "cospherical Hopf category" and
\operatorname{2Rep}(\tilde{C};\tilde{R})
becomes pivotal in the sense of
[
DR18
]
.

\Diamond

5.2.2
Writhing

A direct computation with the 2-
R
-matrix condition
(\tilde{\epsilon}\otimes 1)\tilde{R}=\operatorname{id}=(1\otimes\tilde{\epsilon})\tilde{R}
shows that the composite functor
c_{{\cal D},{\cal D}^{*}}\circ\operatorname{cev}_{\cal D}:{\cal I}\rightarrow{\cal D}^{*}\boxtimes{\cal D}
has the same (trivial)
\tilde{\cal C}
-module structure as
\operatorname{ev}_{\cal D}^{\dagger}
, and similarly for
c_{{\cal D}^{*},{\cal D}}
. As such, we can find 2-morphisms

K_{\cal D}:c_{{\cal D},{\cal D}^{*}}\circ\operatorname{cev}_{\cal D}\Rightarrow\operatorname{ev}_{\cal D}^{\dagger},\qquad\bar{K}_{\cal D}:\operatorname{cev}_{\cal D}\Rightarrow c_{{\cal D}^{*},{\cal D}}\circ\operatorname{ev}_{\cal D}^{\dagger}

called the
over-/under-writhings
; see fig. 31 of
[
BL03
]
. Note ibid. used
\operatorname{cev}_{{\cal D}^{*}}
instead of
\operatorname{ev}_{\cal D}^{\dagger}
in the writhing, hence we recover their definition provided the dual is involutive and the condition (
4.4
) holds.

Remark 5.3
.

Geometrically, the over-/under-writhings implement a rotation the top portion of a fold. This was interpreted as a null-homotopy witnessing the first Reidemeister move in
[
BL03
]
, but we will not take this perspective here. We shall return to this issue in §
6.1.3
.

\Diamond

We now prove how the second Reidemeister moves can be related to two applications of the writhing.

Proposition 5.2
.

Suppose the writhings are invertible and the objects
\nu,\mu
satisfy

\bar{\nu}=\mu^{T}=(-\otimes-)(\tilde{S}\boxtimes 1)\tilde{R},\qquad\bar{\mu}=\nu^{T}

(5.4)

under orientation reversal, then we have

K_{\cal D}\bullet(c_{{\cal D},{\cal D}^{*}}\circ\bar{K}_{\cal D}^{-1})=e_{c_{{\cal D}^{*},{\cal D}}}\circ\operatorname{ev}_{\cal D}^{\dagger},\qquad(c_{{\cal D}^{*},{\cal D}}\circ K_{\cal D}^{-1})\bullet\bar{K}_{\cal D}=\iota_{c_{{\cal D},{\cal D}^{*}}}\circ\operatorname{cev}_{\cal D}.

We call the 2-
R
-matrix
\tilde{R}
satisfying (
5.4
)
quasi-Hermitian
.
9
9

9

It is clear that a Hermitian
\tilde{R}
(see
Remark
5.1
) is quasi-Hermitian, by applying an antipode
1\boxtimes\tilde{S}
and then a contraction
(-\otimes-)
to the definition
\bar{R}=\tilde{R}^{T}
.

Proof.

We begin by post-composing
\bar{K}_{\cal D}^{-1}
with
c_{{\cal D},{\cal D}^{*}}
to achieve the 2-morphism

k_{\cal D}:C_{{\cal D}^{*},{\cal D}}\circ\operatorname{ev}_{\cal D}^{\dagger}=c_{{\cal D},{\cal D}^{*}}\circ c_{{\cal D}^{*},{\cal D}}\circ\operatorname{ev}_{\cal D}^{\dagger}\xRightarrow{c_{{\cal D},{\cal D}^{*}}\circ\bar{K}_{\cal D}^{-1}}c_{{\cal D},{\cal D}^{*}}\circ\operatorname{cev}_{\cal D}\xRightarrow{K_{\cal D}}\operatorname{ev}_{\cal D}^{\dagger}.

(5.5)

If (
5.4
) holds, then the same argument as from
Remark
5.1
implies that there is a(n invertible) 2-morphism
c_{{\cal D}^{*},{\cal D}}\cong c_{{\cal D},{\cal D}^{*}}^{\dagger}
. This allows us to form the commutative triangle

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{c_{{\cal D},{\cal D}^{*}}\circ\operatorname{cev}_{\cal D}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{ev}_{\cal D}^{\dagger}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{c_{{\cal D}^{*},{\cal D}}^{\dagger}\circ c_{{\cal D}^{*},{\cal D}}\circ\operatorname{ev}_{\cal D}^{\dagger}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K_{\cal D}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c_{{\cal D},{\cal D}^{*}}\circ\bar{K}_{\cal D}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces e_{c_{{\cal D}^{*},{\cal D}}}\circ\operatorname{ev}_{\cal D}^{\dagger}}

(5.6)

which states that the composition in (
5.5
) is nothing but
e_{c_{{\cal D}^{*},{\cal D}}}\circ\operatorname{ev}_{\cal D}^{\dagger}
. A completely analogous argument holds for the composite

\bar{k}_{\cal D}:\operatorname{cev}_{\cal D}\xRightarrow{\bar{K}_{\cal D}}c_{{\cal D}^{*},{\cal D}}\circ\operatorname{ev}_{\cal D}^{\dagger}\xRightarrow{c_{{\cal D}^{*},{\cal D}}\circ K_{\cal D}^{-1}}c_{{\cal D}^{*},{\cal D}}\circ c_{{\cal D},{\cal D}^{*}}\circ\operatorname{cev}_{\cal D}=C_{{\cal D},{\cal D}^{*}}\circ\operatorname{cev}_{\cal D}.

(5.7)

Notice the commutative triangle (
5.6
) is nothing but Lemma 16 in
[
BL03
]
.

∎

Throughout the following, we will often assume that the 2-
R
-matrix
\tilde{R}
is quasi-Hermitian.

Remark 5.4
.

Let
\tilde{C}
denote a cobraided Hopf category, and
\tilde{C}^{\text{m-op}}
its monoidal opposite. Its monoidal product is given by
(-\cdot-)^{T}=(-\cdot-)\sigma
, where
\sigma:\tilde{C}\times\tilde{C}\rightarrow\tilde{C}\times\tilde{C}
is a swapping of factors. As such, quasi-Hermiticity (
5.4
) can be understood as a condition relating the orientation reversal of the object
\nu\in\tilde{C}
with the opposite one
\mu^{T}\in\tilde{C}^{\text{m-op}}
. If a monoidal natural transformation
(-\cdot-)\Rightarrow(-\cdot-)^{T}
exists — that is to say, if
\tilde{C}
were
braided
— then the quasi-Hermitian condition appears as a coherence condition between the cobraiding and the braiding. However, there is nothing in the underlying 4d gauge theory which indicates
\tilde{C}
should have a braiding.

\Diamond

For posterity, we define a few structural 2-morphisms that will play an important role later. Composing the writhes
K_{\cal D},\bar{K}_{\cal D}
respectively with the folds
\operatorname{cev}_{\cal D}^{\dagger},\operatorname{ev}_{\cal D}
yield the 2-morphisms

\displaystyle K_{\cal D}^{\prime}:c_{{\cal D},{\cal D}^{*}}\xRightarrow{c_{{\cal D},{\cal D}^{*}}\circ\iota_{\operatorname{cev}_{\cal D}}}c_{{\cal D},{\cal D}^{*}}\circ(\operatorname{cev}_{\cal D}\circ\operatorname{cev}_{\cal D}^{\dagger})\xRightarrow{K_{\cal D}\circ\operatorname{cev}_{\cal D}^{\dagger}}\operatorname{ev}_{\cal D}^{\dagger}\circ\operatorname{cev}_{\cal D}^{\dagger},

\displaystyle\bar{K}_{\cal D}^{\prime}:\operatorname{cev}_{\cal D}\circ\operatorname{ev}_{\cal D}\xRightarrow{\bar{K}_{\cal D}\circ\operatorname{ev}_{\cal D}}c_{{\cal D}^{*},{\cal D}}\circ\operatorname{ev}_{\cal D}^{\dagger}\circ\operatorname{ev}_{\cal D}\xRightarrow{c_{{\cal D}^{*},{\cal D}}\circ e_{\operatorname{ev}_{\cal D}}}c_{{\cal D}^{*},{\cal D}}.

On the other hand, consider the so-called "double point arc crossing a fold line" 2-morphisms
[
CRS97
]

\displaystyle H_{{\cal D},{\cal A}}

\displaystyle=\Omega_{{\cal D}|{\cal D}^{*}{\cal A}}\bullet c_{\operatorname{cev}_{\cal D}^{\dagger},{\cal A}}\bullet{\cal D}\boxtimes\iota_{c_{{\cal D}^{*},{\cal A}}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad:(\operatorname{cev}_{\cal D}^{\dagger}\boxtimes{\cal A})\circ({\cal D}\boxtimes c_{{\cal D}^{*},{\cal A}}^{\dagger})\Rightarrow({\cal A}\boxtimes\operatorname{cev}_{\cal D}^{\dagger})\circ(c_{{\cal A},{\cal D}}\boxtimes{\cal D}^{*}),

\displaystyle G_{{\cal D},{\cal A}}

\displaystyle=\Omega_{{\cal D}^{*}|{\cal D}{\cal A}}\bullet c_{\operatorname{ev}_{\cal D},{\cal A}}\bullet{\cal D}^{*}\boxtimes\iota_{c_{{\cal D},{\cal A}}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad:(\operatorname{ev}_{\cal D}\boxtimes{\cal A})\circ({\cal D}^{*}\boxtimes c_{{\cal D},{\cal A}}^{\dagger})\Rightarrow({\cal A}\boxtimes\operatorname{ev}_{\cal D})\circ(c_{{\cal D}^{*},{\cal A}}\boxtimes{\cal D}),

which we shall shorten to
fold-crossings
; see fig. 32 of
[
BL03
]
. There are also "dual" versions of these 2-morphisms, denoted by
\bar{H},\bar{G}
, in which the folds are replaced by their appropriate barred versions.

5.3
Rigid dagger structures and the writhing

We now study the compatibility of the writhing 2-morphisms with the rigid duality structure. Let us begin with the fold-crossing maps defined above. Consider the following 2-morphisms

(K\bullet H)_{{\cal D},{\cal A}}=\hbox to251.42pt{\vbox to120.79pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 125.70901pt\lower-60.39594pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {}{}{}{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{}{}{{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{}{{

which expresses the crossing of over-/under-writhings over the folds. Similar constructions can be made for the adjoint writhe-crossings
(K\bullet H^{\dagger}),(\bar{K}\bullet G^{\dagger})
, in which braidings of the form
c_{{\cal D}\boxtimes{\cal D}^{*},{\cal A}},
are replaced with their adjoints
c_{{\cal D}\boxtimes{\cal D}^{*},{\cal A}}^{\dagger}
.

5.3.1
Rigid writhing conditions

We now exhibit the compatibility between the writhing and the rigid duality, under the assumption that
\tilde{R}
is quasi-Hermitian (ie. (
5.4
) holds). These are expressed by the so-called
rigid writhing conditions
(
5.8
), (
5.9
), which we shall now deduce.

Starting from quasi-Hermiticity (
5.4
), we deduce a 2-isomorphism
c_{{\cal D}^{*},{\cal D}}\cong c_{{\cal D},{\cal D}^{*}}^{\dagger}
. Taking the writhe-crossing
(K\bullet H^{\dagger})_{{\cal D},{\cal D}^{*}}
at
{\cal A}={\cal D}^{*}
, we see that the cube corresponding to (
5.2
) — with
F=c_{{\cal D},{\cal D}^{*}}
— allows us to stack
(K\bullet H^{\dagger})_{{\cal D},{\cal D}^{*}}
with
(\bar{H}\bullet\bar{K})_{{\cal D},{\cal D}^{*}}
by use of the associator equivalences:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{({\cal D}{\cal D}^{*}){\cal D}^{*}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\cal D}^{*}({\cal D}{\cal D}^{*})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{({\cal D}^{*}{\cal D}){\cal D}^{*}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{({\cal D}{\cal D}^{*}){\cal D}^{*}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\cal D}^{*}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\cal D}^{*}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{({\cal D}^{*}{\cal D}){\cal D}^{*}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\cal D}^{*}({\cal D}^{*}{\cal D})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\cal D}^{*}({\cal D}^{*}{\cal D})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\cal D}^{*}({\cal D}{\cal D}^{*})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(K\bullet H)_{{\cal D},{\cal A}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\bar{K}\bullet G)_{{\cal D},{\cal A}}}

where we have utilized a shorthand to express
(K\bullet H)_{{\cal D},{\cal A}}
and
(\bar{K}\bullet G)_{{\cal D},{\cal A}}
as square diagrams. Notice crucially that we have kept track of the functors that enter/exit the position
{\cal A}={\cal D}^{*}
.

This is important, as we notice that the compositions of the functors
{\cal D}^{*}\rightarrow{\cal D}^{*}
marked in blue (and respectively those marked in red) are given precisely respectively by the cusp
{(\operatorname{ev}_{{\cal D}}\boxtimes{\cal D}^{*})\circ({\cal D}^{*}\boxtimes\operatorname{cev}_{{\cal D}})}
and its adjoint. We can then apply the snakerators/cusps (
3.13
)

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi_{\cal D}:1_{{\cal D}^{*}}\Rightarrow(\operatorname{ev}_{{\cal D}}\boxtimes{\cal D}^{*})\circ({\cal D}^{*}\boxtimes\operatorname{cev}_{{\cal D}})},\qquad{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\varphi_{\cal D}^{\dagger}:({\cal D}^{*}\boxtimes\operatorname{cev}_{{\cal D}}^{\dagger})\circ(\operatorname{ev}_{{\cal D}}^{\dagger}\boxtimes{\cal D}^{*})\Rightarrow 1_{{\cal D}^{*}}},

colour-coded for clarity, to this diagram.

The unitarity of the snakerator
\varphi_{\cal D}
, which follows from the planar-unitarity of the rigid duality structure described in §
4
. We are then able to form the following (rather convoluted) diagram of 2-morphisms

(5.8)

This diagram comes with another version constructed from
(\bar{K}\bullet\bar{H}^{\dagger})_{{\cal D},{\cal D}}
. Stacking it with
(K\bullet H)_{{\cal D},{\cal D}}
and the unitarity of the snakerator
\varrho_{\cal D}
gives

(5.9)

The commutativity of these diagrams (
5.8
), (
5.9
), together with their dual versions involving
\bar{H},\bar{G}
, are the desired
rigid writhing conditions
.

These conditions involving (
5.8
), (
5.9
) can be understood as framed versions of the writhing coherence axioms given in
[
BL03
]
, Definition 14. Geometrically, these conditions express the equivalence between the two ways in which a writhing can be passed through duality cusps
\varphi,\varrho
; see fig. 33 of
[
BL03
]
. But here, the difference between left and right duals allows us to keep track of the framing — we will see this in §
6.1
, specifically
Corollary
6.2
.

5.3.2
The swallowking equations

Equipped with the writhing, we now construct the diagram which "pastes" the two swallowtail equations together, still under the assumption that
\tilde{R}
is quasi-Hemritian. Consider the braiding map
c_{{\cal D},{\cal D}^{*}}:{\cal D}{\cal D}^{*}\rightarrow{\cal D}^{*}{\cal D}
and its adjoint
c_{{\cal D}^{*},{\cal D}}\cong c_{{\cal D},{\cal D}^{*}}^{\dagger}:{\cal D}{\cal D}^{*}\rightarrow{\cal D}^{*}{\cal D}
. The adjoint-mate of the under-writhe

\bar{K}_{\cal D}^{\prime\dagger}:c_{{\cal D}^{*},{\cal D}}^{\dagger}\cong c_{{\cal D},{\cal D}^{*}}\Rightarrow\operatorname{ev}_{\cal D}^{\dagger}\operatorname{cev}_{\cal D}^{\dagger},

together with the interchangers, allows us to form the following 2-morphism

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\cal D}{\cal D}^{*}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\cal D}^{*}{\cal D}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\cal D}{\cal D}^{*}{\cal D}{\cal D}^{*}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\cal I}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\cal D}{\cal D}^{*}{\cal D}^{*}{\cal D}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\cal D}^{*}{\cal D}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\cal D}{\cal D}^{*}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\upsilon_{\operatorname{cev}_{\cal D},\operatorname{ev}_{\cal D}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\upsilon_{\operatorname{ev}_{\cal D},\operatorname{cev}_{\cal D}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bar{K}_{\cal D}^{\prime\dagger}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K_{\cal D}^{\prime}}

(5.10)

On the quadruples
{\cal D}^{*}{\cal D}{\cal D}^{*}{\cal D}
and
{\cal D}{\cal D}^{*}{\cal D}{\cal D}^{*}
, we also have the following interchangers,

\upsilon_{c_{{\cal D},{\cal D}^{*}},c_{{\cal D},{\cal D}^{*}}}=\hbox to141.32pt{\vbox to46.41pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 70.66121pt\lower-23.20534pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {}{}{}{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{}{}{{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{}{{

\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}},

(5.11)

from which we notice that the top-/bottom-most squares involve the interchangers in the swallowtails
\mathcal{S}_{\cal D},\mathcal{S}^{\prime}_{\cal D}
, and the left-/right-most squares fit into the 2-morphism (
5.10
).

Let
\mathcal{K}_{\cal D}
denote the 2-morphism we obtain from the above diamond by "gluing" its left and right sides with (
5.10
). By subsequently pasting
\mathcal{S}_{\cal D},\mathcal{S}^{\prime}_{\cal D}
to the top/bottom squares, we obtain another 2-morphism which we denote suggestively by
\mathcal{S}_{\cal D}\circ\mathcal{K}_{\cal D}\circ\mathcal{S}^{\prime}_{\cal D}
. An adjoint version of this 2-morphism can also be constructed, which involves
\operatorname{cev}_{\cal D}^{\dagger},\operatorname{ev}_{\cal D}^{\dagger}
and the adjoint of the over-writhe
K_{\cal D}^{\dagger}
. The triviality of these resulting 2-morphisms,

\mathcal{S}_{\cal D}\circ\mathcal{K}_{\cal D}\circ\mathcal{S}^{\prime}_{\cal D}=\operatorname{id}_{c_{{\cal D},{\cal D}^{*}}},\qquad\mathcal{S}^{\dagger}_{\cal D}\circ\mathcal{K}^{\dagger}_{\cal D}\circ\mathcal{S}^{\prime\dagger}_{\cal D}=\operatorname{id}_{c_{{\cal D}^{*},{\cal D}}},

(5.12)

is dubbed
the swallowking equations
. Since this condition glues the two swallowtail 2-morphisms together, the author has named it after the
rat king
phenomenon, where the tails of a group of rats become entangled. We will use this condition later in §
6.11
and §
7
.

6
Ribbon tensor 2-category
\operatorname{2Rep}(\tilde{C};\tilde{R})

Equipped with the structural functors and natural transformations that we have deduced from the braiding and rigidity of
\operatorname{2Rep}(\tilde{C};\tilde{R})
, we now study how these come together in a compatible manner. Following the classic results
[
RT90
,
Wor88
,
Maj11
]
that representations of quantum groups form ribbon tensor categories, we shall leverage the following to develop a notion of a
ribbon tensor 2-category
equipped with adjoints.

The central motivation for this is the following; let
\mathcal{T}
is the ribbon 2-category of 2-tangles
[
BL03
]
, then one can define decorated 2-ribbons as
ribbon 2-functors

\mathcal{T}\rightarrow\operatorname{2Rep}(\tilde{C};\tilde{R})

into
\operatorname{2Rep}(\tilde{C};\tilde{R})
, which serves as an explicit example of the 2-tangle hypothesis in
[
BN96
,
BL98
]
. This moreover allows us to define the 2-Chern-Simons TQFT
Z_{2CS}
through a
higher-skein
perspective (cf.
[
MWW22
,
MWW19
]
), where as a functor
Z_{2CS}
assigns a 3-dimensional manifold the space of embedded 2-ribbons. This idea has been pursued in the sequel
[
Che25a
]
.

Remark 6.1
.

Strictly speaking, the framework developed in
[
Che25
]
a priori only leads to the definition of the
discretized
2-Chern-Simons theory,
Z_{d2CS}
. In this context, 2-tangles are treated as embedded as (2-)graphs into the lattice
\Gamma
, such that its boundaries live on the 1-skeleton
\Gamma^{1}
. The "true" 2-Chern-Simons TQFT by taking a direct limit over the lattice refinements. However, the idea of the 2-tangle hypothesis is that, given the ribbon 2-functors into
\operatorname{2Rep}(\tilde{C};\tilde{R})
, the 2-Chern-Simons TQFT can also directly be obtained through higher-skein theory.

\Diamond

6.1
Tortile objects; the ribbon balancing

We now finally come to the ribbon balancing/twist. Consider first the braiding structure
c_{{\cal D}^{*},\,^{*}{\cal D}}:{\cal D}^{*}\boxtimes\,^{*}{\cal D}\rightarrow\,^{*}{\cal D}\boxtimes{\cal D}^{*}
. For the same reason as described in §
5.2.1
, these are associated with the following quantities

\mathfrak{t}=(-\cdot-)(\tilde{S}^{-1}\otimes\tilde{S})(\tilde{R}^{T})=\mathfrak{t}^{l}+\mathfrak{t}^{r},

which comes with its own nudging equations,

\mathfrak{t}^{l}\cdot\mathfrak{t}^{r}=\mathfrak{t}^{r}\cdot\mathfrak{t}^{l}

following from the compatibility of the cobraiding natural transformation
\tilde{R}
. We call these, specifically
\mathfrak{t}
, the
tortile object
of
\tilde{C}
. Note
\tilde{R}
being quasi-Hermitian does
not
imply
c_{{\cal D}^{*},\,^{*}{\cal D}}^{\dagger}\cong c_{\,{}^{*}{\cal D},{\cal D}^{*}}
, since the tortile object is different from the objects
\nu,\mu
introduced in §
5.2.1
.

From the braiding map
c_{{\cal D}^{*},\,^{*}{\cal D}}
, we define for each object
{\cal D}
the
left-over/right-under balancings

\displaystyle\vartheta_{\cal D}=(\overline{\operatorname{ev}}_{\cal D}\boxtimes{\cal D}^{*})\circ({\cal D}\boxtimes c_{{\cal D}^{*},\,^{*}{\cal D}})\circ(\operatorname{cev}_{\cal D}\boxtimes\,^{*}{\cal D}):\,^{*}{\cal D}\rightarrow{\cal D}^{*}

\displaystyle\bar{\vartheta}_{\cal D}=(\,^{*}{\cal D}\boxtimes{\operatorname{ev}}_{\cal D})\circ(c_{{\cal D}^{*},\,^{*}{\cal D}}\boxtimes{\cal D})\circ({\cal D}^{*}\boxtimes\overline{\operatorname{cev}}_{\cal D}):{\cal D}^{*}\rightarrow\,^{*}{\cal D}.

The naturality of their composites gives, for each
\tilde{C}
-module functor
F:{\cal D}\rightarrow{\cal D}^{\prime}
, the balancing 2-morphisms

One can show (through tedious but straightforward computations) from (
3.11
) and (
3.4
), as well as (
2.2
), (
2.1
), that we have the following balancing equations

\displaystyle\vartheta_{{\cal D}\boxtimes{\cal A}}\cong c_{{\cal D}^{*},{\cal A}^{*}}\circ(\vartheta_{\cal D}\boxtimes\vartheta_{\cal A})\circ c_{\,{}^{*}{\cal A},\,^{*}{\cal D}},

\displaystyle\bar{\vartheta}_{{\cal D}\boxtimes{\cal A}}\cong c_{\,{}^{*}{\cal D},\,^{*}{\cal A}}\circ(\bar{\vartheta}_{\cal D}\boxtimes\bar{\vartheta}_{\cal A})\circ c_{{\cal A}^{*},{\cal D}^{*}}

(6.1)

for each
{\cal D},{\cal A}\in\operatorname{2Rep}(\tilde{C};\tilde{R})
; indeed, (
3.4
) implies that these 2-morphisms are given by the unitary hexagonators, and so are unitary themselves and therefore invertible.

Remark 6.2
.

Notice
\vartheta_{{\cal D}^{*}}:{\cal D}\rightarrow({\cal D}^{*})^{*}
is precisely the functor comparing
{\cal D}
and its double-dual mentioned in
Remark
4.2
. It is then clear that the 2-categorical dimension
\mathfrak{Dim}({\cal D})
admits an action only by the centralizer subcategory
C_{\operatorname{End}({\cal D})}(\vartheta)
, which consist of functors
F\in\operatorname{End}({\cal D})
that commute with
\vartheta_{{\cal D}^{*}}
, and natural transformations that commute with the 2-Drinfel’d modification
\omega_{{\cal D}}
(which we shall introduce soon). The issue raised in
Warning 2.2.5
of
[
DR18
]
can thus be circumvented if
C_{\operatorname{End}({\cal D})}(\vartheta)\simeq\mathsf{Hilb}
is trivial. We call braided rigid tensor 2-categories with this property
maximally imbalanced
.

\Diamond

6.1.1
2-Drinfel’d modifications

Recall
\tilde{S}^{2},\tilde{S}^{-2}:\tilde{C}\rightarrow\tilde{C}
are by hypothesis monoidal autoequivalences. By combining (
3.12
) and
Remark
3.4
, we construct invertible 2-morphisms such that

\overline{\operatorname{c/ev}}_{\cal D}^{**}\Rightarrow\operatorname{c/ev}_{{\cal D}^{*}},\qquad\,^{**}\operatorname{c/ev}_{\cal D}\Rightarrow\overline{\operatorname{c/ev}}_{\,{}^{*}{\cal D}},

(6.2)

where "
\operatorname{c/ev}
" means either
\operatorname{ev}
or
\operatorname{cev}
.

Proposition 6.1
.

There are
2-Drinfel’d modifications

\omega_{\cal D}:\bar{\vartheta}_{\cal D}^{*}\Rightarrow\vartheta_{{\cal D}^{*}},\qquad\bar{\omega}_{\cal D}:\,^{*}\vartheta_{{\cal D}}\Rightarrow\bar{\vartheta}_{\,{}^{*}{\cal D}},

which witness the homotopy between left-over and right-under balancings upon a reversal of the framing.

Proof.

Recall the (horizontal) antipode
\tilde{S}:\tilde{C}\rightarrow\tilde{C}^{\text{m-op},\text{c-op}}
is a (strict) op-monoidal functor
\tilde{S}\circ(-\cdot-)=(-\cdot-)^{\text{op}}\circ(\tilde{S}\otimes\tilde{S})
. Its adjunctions
\tilde{S}^{-1}\circ\tilde{S}\cong 1_{\tilde{C}}\cong\tilde{S}\circ\tilde{S}^{-1}
lead to the following identifications

\tilde{S}\mathfrak{t}\cong(-\cdot-)(\tilde{S}^{2}\otimes 1)\tilde{R},\qquad S^{-1}\mathfrak{t}\cong(-\cdot-)(1\otimes\tilde{S}^{-2})\tilde{R}

on the tortile objects. This in turn induces the invertible (unitary) 2-morphisms

c_{-^{*},-^{*}}:c_{{\cal D}^{*},\,^{*}{\cal D}}^{*}\Rightarrow c_{({\cal D}^{*})^{*},{\cal D}},\qquad c_{\,{}^{*}-,\,^{*}-}:\,^{*}c_{{\cal D}^{*},\,^{*}{\cal D}}\Rightarrow c_{{\cal D},\,^{*}(\,^{*}{\cal D})},

(6.3)

corresponding to the braiding on the the duals
-^{*}
or the pre-duals
\,{}^{*}-
.

By horizontally composing
c_{-^{*},-^{*}}
with the 2-morphisms in
Proposition
3.7
, we have with (
6.2
),

\displaystyle\bar{\vartheta}_{\cal D}^{*}

\displaystyle=\big(\overline{\operatorname{cev}}^{*}_{\cal D}\boxtimes({\cal D}^{*})^{*}\big)\circ({\cal D}^{*}\boxtimes c_{{\cal D}^{*},\,^{*}{\cal D}}^{*})\circ(\operatorname{ev}_{\cal D}^{*}\boxtimes{\cal D})

\displaystyle\Rightarrow\big(\operatorname{ev}_{\cal D}\boxtimes({\cal D}^{*})^{*}\big)\circ({\cal D}^{*}\boxtimes c_{({\cal D}^{*})^{*},{\cal D}})\circ(\overline{\operatorname{cev}}_{\cal D}^{**}\boxtimes{\cal D})

\displaystyle\cong\big(\overline{\operatorname{ev}}_{{\cal D}^{*}}\boxtimes({\cal D}^{*})^{*}\big)\circ({\cal D}^{*}\boxtimes c_{({\cal D}^{*})^{*},{\cal D}})\circ(\operatorname{cev}_{{\cal D}^{*}}\boxtimes{\cal D})=\vartheta_{{\cal D}^{*}},

where we have used the equivalences mentioned in
Remark
3.4
. This defines
\omega_{\cal D}
. The other 2-morphism
\bar{\omega}_{\cal D}
can be constructed in an analogous way.
∎

Moreover, the balancing (
6.1
) and (
6.3
) allow us to achieve the identifications

\omega_{{\cal D}\boxtimes{\cal A}}=c_{-^{*},-^{*}}\circ(\omega_{\cal D}\boxtimes\omega_{\cal A})\circ c_{-^{*},-^{*}},\qquad\bar{\omega}_{{\cal D}\boxtimes{\cal A}}=c_{\,{}^{*}-,\,^{*}-}\circ(\bar{\omega}_{\cal D}\boxtimes\bar{\omega}_{\cal A})\circ c_{\,{}^{*}-,\,^{*}-}

for each
{\cal D},{\cal A}
, hence the 2-Drinfel’d modifications are compatible with the tensor product.

If we suppose for the moment that we have a monoidal pseudonatural isomorphism
-^{****}\simeq\operatorname{id}
on
\operatorname{2Rep}(\tilde{C};\tilde{R})
, then the invertibility of (
6.2
) gives rise to

\overline{\operatorname{c/ev}}_{\cal D}\cong(\overline{\operatorname{c/ev}})^{****}_{\cal D}\cong\operatorname{c/ev}_{{\cal D}^{*}}^{**},

which in essence identifies barred quantities as a "half-way" to the quadruple dual. We will prove the triviality of the quadruple dual in a separate paper.

Remark 6.3
.

An alternative proof of
-^{****}\simeq\operatorname{id}
is to leverage a "categorical" Radford
S^{4}
-formula
10
10

10

Recall that the usual Radford
S^{4}
-formula
[
DDW06
]
states that the action of
S^{4}
in a finite-dimensional Hopf algebra is an inner automorphism by grouplike elements.
proven in
[
ENO04
]
: there exists a
distinguished invertible object

c\in C
in a finite tensor category
C
with a natural isomorphism
\delta:-^{**}\Rightarrow c\otimes\,^{**}-\otimes c^{-1}
— the idea is that the rigid duality
-^{*}
gives rise to an antipode functor on the dual
C^{*}
. Thus it is reasonable to expect a categorical Radford
S^{4}
-formula for the Hopf category
\tilde{C}=\tilde{C}
to hold. One subtlety is that
\tilde{C}
itself is not finite, but it can be made unimodular with the use of a
Haar cointegral
(see §5.2.3 in
[
Che25a
]
).

\Diamond

Due to the naturality of the ribbon balancing functors, an immediate corollary is the following.

Corollary 6.2
.

When
\omega_{\cal D},\bar{\omega}_{\cal D}
are invertible, then for all
\tilde{C}
-module functors
F:{\cal D}\rightarrow{\cal D}^{\prime}
we have

\vartheta_{F^{*}}=\bar{\vartheta}_{F}^{*},\qquad\vartheta_{\,{}^{*}F}=\,^{*}\bar{\vartheta}_{F}.

(6.4)

As such,
\operatorname{End}({\cal I})
is a ribbon tensor category.

Notice these are rigid dagger generalizations of the C8 condition in
[
DR18
]
, Definition 2.2.4.

Remark 6.4
.

The reason we call
\omega_{\cal D},\bar{\omega}_{\cal D}
the "2-Drinfel’d modifications" is the following. In the scenario where
\vartheta,\bar{\vartheta}
are genuine "twists" — ie. (pseudo)natural transformations on the identity on
\operatorname{2Rep}(\tilde{C};\tilde{R})
(which cannot happen unless the duality is involutive) — then
\omega=\bar{\omega}^{-1}
is an invertible modification between these pseudonatural twists. As such, they serve to "change" the pivotal structures on rigid 2-categories, and hence appears in
Remark
6.2
as part of the notion of "maximal imbalancing".

\Diamond

6.1.2
Reidemeister II: double-twist cancellations and the belt-buckle move

In the rigid dagger setting, each of the composites in the definition of the ribbon balancings
\vartheta_{\cal D},\bar{\vartheta}_{\cal D}
are planar-unitary, and hence are themselves planar-unitary. As such they admit folds against their adjoints. The folds

e_{\vartheta_{\cal D}}:\vartheta_{\cal D}^{\dagger}\circ\vartheta_{\cal D}\Rightarrow 1_{\,{}^{*}{\cal D}},\qquad e_{\bar{\vartheta}_{\cal D}}:\bar{\vartheta}_{\cal D}^{\dagger}\circ\bar{\vartheta}_{\cal D}\Rightarrow 1_{{\cal D}^{*}},

in particular, are known as the
Kauffman double twist cancellations
; see fig. 55 (d) in
[
BMS24
]
. These adjunctions witness the cancellation of twists that live on the
same side
of the tangle. We call such twist cancellations "of the first type".

As the name suggests, there is another type of double twist cancellation, in which the twists lie on different sides of the tangle.

Proposition 6.3
.

Suppose
\tilde{R}
is quasi-Hemritian (
5.4
). There are 2-morphisms

\kappa_{\cal D}:\big({\cal D}\boxtimes(\vartheta_{\cal D}\circ\bar{\vartheta}_{\cal D})\big)\circ\operatorname{cev}_{\cal D}\Rightarrow\operatorname{cev}_{\cal D},\qquad\bar{\kappa}_{\cal D}:\big((\bar{\vartheta}_{\cal D}\circ\vartheta_{\cal D})\boxtimes{\cal D}\big)\circ\overline{\operatorname{cev}}_{\cal D}\Rightarrow\overline{\operatorname{cev}}_{\cal D}

which witness the null-homotopy of distinct twist types on a fold.

Proof.

To prove this, we need to introduce the so-called
belt-buckle moves
. Geometrically, these are isotopies which "drag" twists on the
outside
(and only the outside!) of a fold to the top. They are implemented by the following 2-morphisms

\mathrm{P}_{\cal D}:({\cal D}\boxtimes\bar{\vartheta}_{\cal D})\circ\operatorname{cev}_{\cal D}\Rightarrow c_{\,{}^{*}{\cal D},{\cal D}}\circ\overline{\operatorname{cev}}_{{\cal D}},\qquad\mathrm{Q}_{\cal D}:(\vartheta_{{\cal D}}\boxtimes{\cal D})\circ\overline{\operatorname{cev}}_{\cal D}\Rightarrow c_{{\cal D},{\cal D}^{*}}\circ\operatorname{cev}_{\cal D}.

We shall describe the construction of
\mathrm{P}_{\cal D}
in our current context;
\mathrm{Q}_{\cal D}
can be obtained in a similar manner. From the quasi-Hermitian hypothesis
c_{\,{}^{*}{\cal D},{\cal D}}^{\dagger}\cong c_{{\cal D},\,^{*}{\cal D}}
, we have an adjunction

}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{33.67082pt}{-49.64572pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform=matrix(1.0 0.0 0.0 1.0 46.59 -68.69)} \pgfsys@hbox{58}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
